pure MC1 reference semantics.\n\n`MergeRel` relates an exact base program `O`, two variants `A` and `B` and a\nsupplied candidate `M` that share one complete ordered input stack type and one\ncomplete ordered output stack type.  For every well-typed input vector the four\nprograms must return normally and the candidate's full output stack must be the\nper-observable three-way merge of the variants against the base.  The relation\nquantifies over every normal return, so it does not assume a deterministic\nevaluator; for the admitted fragment the reference semantics is deterministic\nper concrete subject, which the fixture theorems below check explicitly.\n\nValue equality is structural: `I64` is a 64-bit bit pattern with wrapping\narithmetic, so a mathematical-integer reading is never used.\n\nThe module also states and proves the terminating-fragment theorem.  The\nfragment proved here is a strict sub-fragment of the design's: leaf code is\nstraight-line first-order dataflow with no invocation at all, and outer code\nadds statically embedded leaf blocks, quotation of first-order data and the\neliminators `dip`/`run`/`case`/`if`/`listCase`.  Every invoked program is thus\na leaf, so termination follows from the finite program, the finite embedding\ntree and the total builtins - never from purity or an empty effect set.  The\ndesign's named definitions and deeper quotations are not modeled here; that\nnarrowing is recorded in the change's design.\n\nThis file proves the source-model obligation.  It is not a claim about Wasm\nexecution, a compiler, the host or an installed merge tool.\n-/\n\nnamespace NobleContracts\n\n/-! ## Observables -/\n\n/-- First-order data types: the only observable types of `SMERGE/1`. -/\ninductive ObservableTy : Ty → Prop where\n  | unit : ObservableTy .unit\n  | bool : ObservableTy .bool\n  | i64 : ObservableTy .i64\n  | text : ObservableTy .text\n  | pair {a b : Ty} : ObservableTy a → ObservableTy b → ObservableTy (.pair a b)\n  | sum {a b : Ty} : ObservableTy a → ObservableTy b → ObservableTy (.sum a b)\n  | list {a : Ty} : ObservableTy a → ObservableTy (.list a)\n\n/-- First-order data values; no program, quotation or syntax value occurs. -/\ninductive FirstOrderValue : Value → Prop where\n  | unit : FirstOrderValue .unit\n  | bool {b : Bool} : FirstOrderValue (.bool b)\n  | i64 {n : BitVec 64} : FirstOrderValue (.i64 n)\n  | text {s : String} : FirstOrderValue (.text s)\n  | pair {a b : Value} : FirstOrderValue a → FirstOrderValue b →\n      FirstOrderValue (.pair a b)\n  | inl {v : Value} : FirstOrderValue v → FirstOrderValue (.inl v)\n  | inr {v : Value} : FirstOrderValue v → FirstOrderValue (.inr v)\n  | list {vs : List Value} : (∀ v ∈ vs, FirstOrderValue v) →\n      FirstOrderValue (.list vs)\n\n/-! ## The per-observable three-way merge -/\n\n/-- `MergeVal o a b m` is the three-way merge of one observable: the base value\n`o`, the variant values `a` and `b`, and the merged value `m`. : Value) : Prop := ∀ m : Value, ¬ MergeVal o a b m\n\ntheorem mergeVal_functional {o a b m m' : Value}\n    (left : MergeVal o a b m) (right : MergeVal o a b m') : m = m' := by\n  cases left with\n  | unchanged =>\n      cases right with\n      | unchanged => rfl\n      | first h => exact absurd rfl h\n      | second h => exact absurd rfl h\n      | agree h _ _ => exact absurd rfl h\n  | first h Or.inl ⟨b, .second hb⟩\n  · by_cases hb : b = o\n    · cases hb\n      exact Or.inl ⟨a, .first ha⟩\n    · by_cases hab : a = b\n      · cases hab\n        exact Or.inl ⟨a, .agree ha hb rfl⟩\n      · stackTyped_append_pair {s : Stack} {i : List Ty} {a b : Ty}\n    (h : StackTyped s (i ++ [a, b])) :\n    ∃ t x y, s = t ++ [x, y] ∧ StackTyped t i ∧ HasType x a ∧ HasType y b := by\n  obtain ⟨t, u, rfl, ht, hu⟩ := stackTyped_split h\n  obtain ⟨x, y, rfl, hx, hy⟩ := stackTyped_pair_shape hu\n  exact ⟨t, x, y, rfl, ht, hx, hy⟩\n\ntheorem stackTyped_append_three {s : Stack} {i : List Ty} {a b c : Ty}\n    (h : StackTyped s (i ++ [a, b, c])) :\n    ∃ t x y z, s = t ++ [x, y, z] ∧ StackTyped t i ∧ HasType x a ∧\n      HasType y b ∧ HasType z c := by\n  obtain ⟨t, u, rfl, ht, hu⟩ := stackTyped_split h\n  obtain ⟨x, v, rfl, hx, hv⟩ := (stackTyped_cons_iff _ _ _).mp hu\n  obtain ⟨y, z, rfl, hy, hz⟩ := stackTyped_pair_shape hv\n  exact ⟨t, x, y, z, rfl, ht, hx, hy, hz⟩\n\ntheorem hasType_program_shape {v : Value} {i o : List Ty}\n    (h : HasType v (.program i o)) :\n    ∃ p, v = .program p ∧ CodeTyped p i o := by\n  cases h with\n  | program hcode => exact ⟨_, rfl, hcode⟩\n\ntheorem hasType_pair_shape {v : Value} {a b : Ty} (h : HasType v (.pair a b)) :\n    ∃ x y, v = .pair x y ∧ HasType x a ∧ HasType y b := by\n  cases h with\n  | pair hx hy => exact ⟨_, _, rfl, hx, hy⟩\n\ntheorem hasType_sum_shape {v : Value} {a b : Ty} (h : HasType v (.sum a b)) :\n    (∃ x, v = .inl x ∧ HasType x a) ∨ (∃ y, v = .inr y ∧ HasType y b) := by\n  cases h with\n  | inl hx => exact .inl ⟨_, rfl, hx⟩\n  | inr hy => exact .inr ⟨_, rfl, hy⟩\n\ntheorem hasType_list_shape {v : Value} {a : Ty} (h : HasType v (.list a)) :\n    ∃ vs, v = .list vs ∧ ∀ x ∈ vs, HasType x a := by\n  cases h with\n  | list hv => exact ⟨_, rfl, hv⟩\n\n/-! ## The terminating fragment -/\n\nmutual\n  /-- Straight-line dataflow.  No program value is invoked, reflected or\n  composed, so one op consumes one constructor.  A literal may quote a leaf\n  value; a literal program is admitted only when its body is a leaf. -/\n  inductive CoreOp : Op → Prop where\n    | lit {v : Value} : LeafValue v → CoreOp (.lit v)\n    | dup : CoreOp (.word 0)\n    | drop : CoreOp (.word 1)\n    | swap : CoreOp (.word 2)\n    | add : CoreOp (.word 4)\n    | sub : CoreOp (.word 5)\n    | mul : CoreOp (.word 6)\n    | equals : CoreOp (.word 7)\n    | unit : CoreOp (.word 12)\n    | pair : CoreOp (.word 13)\n    | unpair : CoreOp (.word 14)\n    | inl : CoreOp (.word 15)\n    | inr : CoreOp (.word 16)\n    | nil : CoreOp (.word 19)\n    | cons : CoreOp (.word 20)\n\n  inductive LeafCode : List Op → Prop where\n    | nil : LeafCode []\n    | cons {op : Op} {ops : List Op} : CoreOp op → LeafCode ops → LeafCode (op :: ops)\n\n  /-- A value every program inside which is a leaf: first-order data possibly\n  containing leaf quotations and leaf blocks. -/\n  inductive LeafValue : Value → Prop where\n    | unit : LeafValue .unit\n    | bool {b : Bool} : LeafValue (.bool b)\n    | i64 {n : BitVec 64} : LeafValue (.i64 n)\n    | text {s : String} : LeafValue (.text s)\n    | pair {a b : Value} : LeafValue a → LeafValue b → LeafValue (.pair a b)\n    | inl {v : Value} : LeafValue v → LeafValue (.inl v)\n    | inr {v : Value} : LeafValue v → LeafValue (.inr v)\n    | list {vs : List Value} : (∀ v ∈ vs, LeafValue v) → LeafValue (.list vs)\n    | program {p : List Op} : LeafCode p → LeafValue (.program p)\nend\n\n/-- Every stack value is a leaf value. -/\ndef AllLeaf (s : Stack) : Prop := ∀ v ∈ s, LeafValue v\n\ntheorem firstOrder_leafValue {v : Value} (h : FirstOrderValue v) : LeafValue v := by\n  cases h with\n  | unit => exact .unit\n  | bool => exact .bool\n  | i64 => exact .i64\n  | text => exact .text\n  | pair ha hb => exact .pair (firstOrder_leafValue ha) (firstOrder_leafValue hb)\n  | inl hv => exact .inl (firstOrder_leafValue hv)\n  | inr hv => exact .inr (firstOrder_leafValue hv)\n  | list hvs => exact .list (fun v hv => firstOrder_leafValue (hvs v hv))\n\ntheorem leafValue_list_shape {vs : List Value} (h : LeafValue (.list vs)) :\n    ∀ v ∈ vs, LeafValue v := by\n  cases h with\n  | list hv => exact hv\n\ntheorem leafValue_program_shape {p : List Op} (h : LeafValue (.program p)) :\n    LeafCode p := by\n  cases h with\n  | program hcode => exact hcode\n\ntheorem allLeaf_nil : AllLeaf [] := by intro v hv; cases hv\n\ntheorem allLeaf_cons {v : Value} {s : Stack} (hv : LeafValue v) (hs : AllLeaf s) :\n    AllLeaf (v :: s) := by\n  intro w hw\n  cases hw with\n  | head => exact hv\n  | tail _ h => exact hs w h\n\ntheorem allLeaf_append_iff {s t : Stack} : AllLeaf (s ++ t) ↔ AllLeaf s ∧ AllLeaf t := by\n  constructor\n  · intro h\n    exact ⟨fun v hv => h v (List.mem_append_left _ hv),\n      fun v hv => h v (List.mem_append_right _ hv)⟩\n  · rintro ⟨hs, ht⟩ v hv\n    cases List.mem_append.mp hv with\n    | inl h => exact hs v h\n    | inr h => exact ht v h\n\ntheorem allLeaf_append {s t : Stack} (hs : AllLeaf s) (ht : AllLeaf t) :\n    AllLeaf (s ++ t) := allLeaf_append_iff.mpr ⟨hs, ht⟩\n\ntheorem allLeaf_singleton {v : Value} (hv : LeafValue v) : AllLeaf [v] :=\n  allLeaf_cons hv allLeaf_nil\n\ntheorem allLeaf_singleton_inv {v : Value} (h : AllLeaf [v]) : LeafValue v :=\n  h v (by simp)\n\ntheorem allLeaf_pair_inv {x y : Value} {rest : Stack}\n    (h : AllLeaf (x :: y :: rest)) : LeafValue x ∧ LeafValue y ∧ AllLeaf rest := by\n  refine ⟨h x (by simp), h y (by simp), ?_⟩\n  intro v hv\n  exact h v (by simp [hv])\n\ntheorem leafValue_pair_inv {x y : Value} (h : LeafValue (.pair x y)) :\n    LeafValue x ∧ LeafValue y := by\n  cases h with\n  | pair hx hy => exact ⟨hx, hy⟩\n\ntheorem leafValue_inl_inv {x : Value} (h : LeafValue (.inl x)) : LeafValue x := by\n  cases h with\n  | inl hv => exact hv\n\ntheorem leafValue_inr_inv {x : Value} (h : LeafValue (.inr x)) : LeafValue x := by\n  cases h with\n  | inr hv => exact hv\n\ntheorem core_step {op : Op} {i o : List Ty} {s : Stack} (hcore : CoreOp op)\n    (htyped : OpTyped op i o) (hstack : StackTyped s i) (hall : AllLeaf s) :\n    ∃ t, Exec [op] s t ∧ StackTyped t o ∧ AllLeaf t := by\n  cases hcore with\n  | lit hval =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | lit hv =>\n          refine ⟨s ++ [_], exec_lit_iff.mpr rfl, stackTyped_append hstack (.cons hv .nil),\n            allLeaf_append hall (allLeaf_singleton hval)⟩\n  | dup =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | dup =>\n          obtain ⟨t, x, rfl, ht, hx⟩ := stackTyped_append_singleton hstack\n          have hallx := allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall\n          have hxl : LeafValue x := allLeaf_singleton_inv hallx.2\n          refine ⟨t ++ [x, x], exec_dup_iff.mpr rfl, ?_, ?_⟩\n          · simpa [List.append_assoc] using\n              stackTyped_append (stackTyped_append ht (.cons hx .nil)) (.cons hx .nil)\n          · exact allLeaf_append hallx.1 (allLeaf_cons hxl (allLeaf_singleton hxl))\n  | drop =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | drop =>\n          obtain ⟨t, x, rfl, ht, _⟩ := stackTyped_append_singleton hstack\n          exact ⟨t, exec_drop_iff.mpr rfl, ht, (allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall).1⟩\n  | swap =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | swap =>\n          obtain ⟨t, x, y, rfl, ht, hx, hy⟩ := stackTyped_append_pair hstack\n          have hallx := allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall\n          obtain ⟨hxl, hyl, _⟩ := allLeaf_pair_inv hallx.2\n          refine ⟨t ++ [y, x], exec_swap_iff.mpr rfl, ?_, ?_⟩\n          · simpa [List.append_assoc] using\n              stackTyped_append (stackTyped_append ht (.cons hy .nil)) (.cons hx .nil)\n          · exact allLeaf_append hallx.1\n              (allLeaf_append (allLeaf_singleton hyl) (allLeaf_singleton hxl))\n  | add =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | add =>\n          obtain ⟨t, x, y, rfl, ht, hx, hy⟩ := stackTyped_append_pair hstack\n          obtain ⟨xa, rfl⟩ := (hasType_i64_iff _).mp hx\n          obtain ⟨ya, rfl⟩ := (hasType_i64_iff _).mp hy\n          refine ⟨t ++ [.i64 (xa + ya)], exec_add_iff.mpr rfl, ?_, ?_⟩\n          · simpa [List.append_assoc] using stackTyped_append ht (.cons .i64 .nil)\n          · exact allLeaf_append (allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall).1 (allLeaf_singleton .i64)\n  | sub =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | sub =>\n          obtain ⟨t, x, y, rfl, ht, hx, hy⟩ := stackTyped_append_pair hstack\n          obtain ⟨xa, rfl⟩ := (hasType_i64_iff _).mp hx\n          obtain ⟨ya, rfl⟩ := (hasType_i64_iff _).mp hy\n          refine ⟨t ++ [.i64 (xa - ya)], exec_sub_iff.mpr rfl, ?_, ?_⟩\n          · simpa [List.append_assoc] using stackTyped_append ht (.cons .i64 .nil)\n          · exact allLeaf_append (allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall).1 (allLeaf_singleton .i64)\n  | mul =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | mul =>\n          obtain ⟨t, x, y, rfl, ht, hx, hy⟩ := stackTyped_append_pair hstack\n          obtain ⟨xa, rfl⟩ := (hasType_i64_iff _).mp hx\n          obtain ⟨ya, rfl⟩ := (hasType_i64_iff _).mp hy\n          refine ⟨t ++ [.i64 (xa * ya)], exec_mul_iff.mpr rfl, ?_, ?_⟩\n          · simpa [List.append_assoc] using stackTyped_append ht (.cons .i64 .nil)\n          · exact allLeaf_append (allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall).1 (allLeaf_singleton .i64)\n  | equals =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | equals =>\n          obtain ⟨t, x, y, rfl, ht, hx, hy⟩ := stackTyped_append_pair hstack\n          obtain ⟨xa, rfl⟩ := (hasType_i64_iff _).mp hx\n          obtain ⟨ya, rfl⟩ := (hasType_i64_iff _).mp hy\n          refine ⟨t ++ [.bool (xa == ya)], exec_equals_iff.mpr rfl, ?_, ?_⟩\n          · simpa [List.append_assoc] using stackTyped_append ht (.cons .bool .nil)\n          · exact allLeaf_append (allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall).1 (allLeaf_singleton .bool)\n  | unit =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | unit =>\n          refine ⟨s ++ [.unit], exec_unit_iff.mpr rfl, ?_, ?_⟩\n          · simpa using stackTyped_append hstack (.cons .unit .nil)\n          · exact allLeaf_append hall (allLeaf_singleton .unit)\n  | pair =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | pair =>\n          obtain ⟨t, x, y, rfl, ht, hx, hy⟩ := stackTyped_append_pair hstack\n          have hallx := allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall\n          obtain ⟨hxl, hyl, _⟩ := allLeaf_pair_inv hallx.2\n          refine ⟨t ++ [.pair x y], exec_pair_iff.mpr rfl, ?_, ?_⟩\n          · simpa [List.append_assoc] using stackTyped_append ht (.cons (.pair hx hy) .nil)\n          · exact allLeaf_append hallx.1 (allLeaf_singleton (.pair hxl hyl))\n  | unpair =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | unpair =>\n          obtain ⟨t, x, rfl, ht, hx⟩ := stackTyped_append_singleton hstack\n          obtain ⟨a, b, rfl, ha, hb⟩ := hasType_pair_shape hx\n          have hallx := (allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall).2\n          obtain ⟨hla, hlb⟩ := leafValue_pair_inv (allLeaf_singleton_inv hallx)\n          refine ⟨t ++ [a, b], exec_unpair_iff.mpr rfl, ?_, ?_⟩\n          · simpa [List.append_assoc] using\n              stackTyped_append (stackTyped_append ht (.cons ha .nil)) (.cons hb .nil)\n          · exact allLeaf_append (allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall).1\n              (allLeaf_append (allLeaf_singleton hla) (allLeaf_singleton hlb))\n  | inl =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | inl =>\n          obtain ⟨t, x, rfl, ht, hx⟩ := stackTyped_append_singleton hstack\n          have hallx := (allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall).2\n          have hxl : LeafValue x := allLeaf_singleton_inv hallx\n          refine ⟨t ++ [.inl x], exec_inl_iff.mpr rfl, ?_, ?_⟩\n          · simpa [List.append_assoc] using stackTyped_append ht (.cons (.inl hx) .nil)\n          · exact allLeaf_append (allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall).1\n              (allLeaf_singleton (.inl hxl))\n  | inr =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | inr =>\n          obtain ⟨t, x, rfl, ht, hx⟩ := stackTyped_append_singleton hstack\n          have hallx := (allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall).2\n          have hxl : LeafValue x := allLeaf_singleton_inv hallx\n          refine ⟨t ++ [.inr x], exec_inr_iff.mpr rfl, ?_, ?_⟩\n          · simpa [List.append_assoc] using stackTyped_append ht (.cons (.inr hx) .nil)\n          · exact allLeaf_append (allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall).1\n              (allLeaf_singleton (.inr hxl))\n  | nil =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | nil =>\n          refine ⟨s ++ [.list []], exec_nil_word_iff.mpr rfl, ?_, ?_⟩\n          · refine stackTyped_append hstack (.cons ?_ .nil)\n            exact .list (by intro v hv; cases hv)\n          · exact allLeaf_append hall (allLeaf_singleton (.list (by intro v hv; cases hv)))\n  | cons =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | cons =>\n          obtain ⟨t, x, y, rfl, ht, hx, hy⟩ := stackTyped_append_pair hstack\n          obtain ⟨vs, rfl, hvs⟩ := hasType_list_shape hy\n          have hallx := allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall\n          obtain ⟨hxl, hlist, _⟩ := allLeaf_pair_inv hallx.2\n          have htail : ∀ z ∈ vs, LeafValue z := leafValue_list_shape hlist\n          refine ⟨t ++ [.list (x :: vs)], exec_cons_iff.mpr rfl, ?_, ?_⟩\n          · refine stackTyped_append ht (.cons (.list ?_) .nil)\n            intro z hz\n            cases hz with\n            | head => exact hx\n            | tail _ h => exact hvs z h\n          · exact allLeaf_append hallx.1 (allLeaf_singleton (.list (by\n              intro z hz\n              cases hz with\n              | head => exact hxl\n              | tail _ h => exact htail z h)))\n\ntheorem leaf_exec {c : List Op} {i o : List Ty} {s : Stack} (htyped : CodeTyped c i o)\n    (hleaf : LeafCode c) (hstack : StackTyped s i) (hall : AllLeaf s) :\n    ∃ t, Exec c s t ∧ StackTyped t o ∧ AllLeaf t := by\n  revert i o s\n  induction c with\n  | nil =>\n      intro i o s htyped hstack hall\n      cases htyped with\n      | nil => exact ⟨s, exec_nil_iff.mpr rfl, hstack, hall⟩\n  | cons op ops ih =>\n      intro i o s htyped hstack hall\n      cases htyped with\n      | cons hop hrest =>\n          cases hleaf with\n          | cons hcore htail =>\n              obtain ⟨m, hm, hmt, hallm⟩ := core_step hcore hop hstack hall\n              obtain ⟨t, ht, htt, hallt⟩ := ih htail hrest hmt hallm\n              exact ⟨t, exec_append_iff.mpr ⟨m, hm, ht⟩, htt, hallt⟩\n\ntheorem allLeaf_three_inv {a b c : Value} {rest : Stack}\n    (h : AllLeaf (a :: b :: c :: rest)) :\n    LeafValue a ∧ LeafValue b ∧ LeafValue c ∧ AllLeaf rest := by\n  refine ⟨h a (by simp), h b (by simp), h c (by simp), ?_⟩\n  intro v hv\n  exact h v (by simp [hv])\n\n/-! Outer code: leaf code, statically embedded leaf blocks, quotation and the\neliminators whose programs are stack values.  Quotation has no static payload:\nthe quoted value is a stack value, so `AllLeaf` requires it to be a leaf and\nthe quoted body is admitted only then.  Every reachable program value is thus a\nleaf, which is the fragment's termination premise. -/\ninductive OuterOp : Op → Prop where\n  | core {op : Op} : CoreOp op → OuterOp op\n  | block {p : List Op} : LeafCode p → OuterOp (.block p)\n  | quote : OuterOp (.word 8)\n  | dip : OuterOp (.word 3)\n  | run : OuterOp (.word 10)\n  | caseWord : OuterOp (.word 17)\n  | ifWord : OuterOp (.word 18)\n  | listCase : OuterOp (.word 21)\n\ninductive OuterCode : List Op → Prop where\n  | nil : OuterCode []\n  | cons {op : Op} {ops : List Op} : OuterOp op → OuterCode ops → OuterCode (op :: ops)\n\ntheorem outer_step {op : Op} {i o : List Ty} {s : Stack} (houter : OuterOp op)\n    (htyped : OpTyped op i o) (hstack : StackTyped s i) (hall : AllLeaf s) :\n    ∃ t, Exec [op] s t ∧ StackTyped t o ∧ AllLeaf t := by\n  cases houter with\n  | core hcore => exact core_step hcore htyped hstack hall\n  | block hleaf =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | block hcode =>\n          refine ⟨s ++ [.program _], exec_block_iff.mpr rfl, ?_, ?_⟩\n          · exact stackTyped_append hstack (.cons (.program hcode) .nil)\n          · exact allLeaf_append hall (allLeaf_singleton (.program hleaf))\n  | quote =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | quote =>\n          obtain ⟨t, v, rfl, ht, hv⟩ := stackTyped_append_singleton hstack\n          refine ⟨t ++ [.program [.lit v]], exec_quote_iff.mpr rfl, ?_, ?_⟩\n          · exact stackTyped_append ht (.cons (.program (.cons (.lit hv) .nil)) .nil)\n          · exact allLeaf_append (allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall).1\n              (allLeaf_singleton (.program (.cons (.lit (allLeaf_singleton_inv (allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall).2)) .nil)))\n  | dip =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | dip =>\n          obtain ⟨t, v, prog, rfl, ht, hv, hprog⟩ := stackTyped_append_pair hstack\n          obtain ⟨p, rfl, hcode⟩ := hasType_program_shape hprog\n          have hallx := allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall\n          obtain ⟨hvl, hprogl, _⟩ := allLeaf_pair_inv hallx.2\n          obtain ⟨u, hu, htu, hallu⟩ :=\n            leaf_exec hcode (leafValue_program_shape hprogl) ht hallx.1\n          refine ⟨u ++ [v], exec_dip_iff.mpr ⟨u, hu, rfl⟩, ?_, ?_⟩\n          · exact stackTyped_append htu (.cons hv .nil)\n          · exact allLeaf_append hallu (allLeaf_singleton hvl)\n  | run =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | run =>\n          obtain ⟨t, prog, rfl, ht, hprog⟩ := stackTyped_append_singleton hstack\n          obtain ⟨p, rfl, hcode⟩ := hasType_program_shape hprog\n          have hallx := allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall\n          obtain ⟨u, hu, htu, hallu⟩ :=\n            leaf_exec hcode (leafValue_program_shape (allLeaf_singleton_inv hallx.2)) ht hallx.1\n          exact ⟨u, exec_run_iff.mpr hu, htu, hallu⟩\n  | caseWord =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | caseWord =>\n          obtain ⟨t, value, p, q, rfl, ht, hv, hp, hq⟩ := stackTyped_append_three hstack\n          obtain ⟨hvl, hpl, hql, _⟩ := allLeaf_three_inv (allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall).2\n          obtain ⟨pc, rfl, hcodep⟩ := hasType_program_shape hp\n          obtain ⟨qc, rfl, hcodeq⟩ := hasType_program_shape hq\n          rcases hasType_sum_shape hv with ⟨x, rfl, hx⟩ | ⟨y, rfl, hy⟩\n          · obtain ⟨u, hu, htu, hallu⟩ := leaf_exec hcodep\n              (leafValue_program_shape hpl) (stackTyped_append ht (.cons hx .nil))\n              (allLeaf_append (allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall).1 (allLeaf_singleton (leafValue_inl_inv hvl)))\n            exact ⟨u, exec_case_left_iff.mpr hu, htu, hallu⟩\n          · obtain ⟨u, hu, htu, hallu⟩ := leaf_exec hcodeq\n              (leafValue_program_shape hql) (stackTyped_append ht (.cons hy .nil))\n              (allLeaf_append (allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall).1 (allLeaf_singleton (leafValue_inr_inv hvl)))\n            exact ⟨u, exec_case_right_iff.mpr hu, htu, hallu⟩\n  | ifWord =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | ifWord =>\n          obtain ⟨t, value, p, q, rfl, ht, hv, hp, hq⟩ := stackTyped_append_three hstack\n          have hallx := allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall\n          obtain ⟨hvl, hpl, hql, _⟩ := allLeaf_three_inv hallx.2\n          obtain ⟨pc, rfl, hcodep⟩ := hasType_program_shape hp\n          obtain ⟨qc, rfl, hcodeq⟩ := hasType_program_shape hq\n          obtain ⟨b, rfl⟩ := (hasType_bool_iff _).mp hv\n          cases b with\n          | true =>\n              obtain ⟨u, hu, htu, hallu⟩ := leaf_exec hcodep\n                (leafValue_program_shape hpl) ht hallx.1\n              exact ⟨u, exec_if_true_iff.mpr hu, htu, hallu⟩\n          | false =>\n              obtain ⟨u, hu, htu, hallu⟩ := leaf_exec hcodeq\n                (leafValue_program_shape hql) ht hallx.1\n              exact ⟨u, exec_if_false_iff.mpr hu, htu, hallu⟩\n  | listCase =>\n      cases htyped with\n      | listCase =>\n          obtain ⟨t, value, p, q, rfl, ht, hv, hp, hq⟩ := stackTyped_append_three hstack\n          have hallx := allLeaf_append_iff.mp hall\n          obtain ⟨hvl, hpl, hql, _⟩ := allLeaf_three_inv hallx.2\n          obtain ⟨pc, rfl, hcodep⟩ := hasType_program_shape hp\n          obtain ⟨qc, rfl, hcodeq⟩ := hasType_program_shape hq\n          obtain ⟨vs, rfl, hvs⟩ := hasType_list_shape hv\n          have hallvs : ∀ z ∈ vs, LeafValue z := leafValue_list_shape hvl\n          cases vs with\n          | nil =>\n              obtain ⟨u, hu, htu, hallu⟩ := leaf_exec hcodep\n                (leafValue_program_shape hpl) ht hallx.1\n              exact ⟨u, exec_list_nil_iff.mpr hu, htu, hallu⟩\n          | cons v vs =>\n              have htyv : HasType v _ := hvs v (by simp)\n              have htylist : HasType (.list vs) _ :=\n                .list (vs := vs) (fun z hz => hvs z (by simp [hz]))\n              have hvleaf : LeafValue v := hallvs v (by simp)\n              have hlistleaf : LeafValue (.list vs) :=\n                .list (vs := vs) (fun z hz => hallvs z (by simp [hz]))\n              obtain ⟨u, hu, htu, hallu⟩ := leaf_exec hcodeq\n                (leafValue_program_shape hql)\n                (stackTyped_append ht (.cons htyv (.cons htylist .nil)))\n                (allLeaf_append hallx.1 (allLeaf_cons hvleaf (allLeaf_singleton hlistleaf)))\n              exact ⟨u, exec_list_cons_iff.mpr hu, htu, hallu⟩\n\ntheorem outer_exec {c : List Op} {i o : List Ty} {s : Stack} (htyped : CodeTyped c i o)\n    (hcode : OuterCode c) (hstack : StackTyped s i) (hall : AllLeaf s) :\n    ∃ t, Exec c s t ∧ StackTyped t o ∧ AllLeaf t := by\n  revert i o s\n  induction c with\n  | nil =>\n      intro i o s htyped hstack hall\n      cases htyped with\n      | nil => exact ⟨s, exec_nil_iff.mpr rfl, hstack, hall⟩\n  | cons op ops ih =>\n      intro i o s htyped hstack hall\n      cases htyped with\n      | cons hop hrest =>\n          cases hcode with\n          | cons houter htail =>\n              obtain ⟨m, hm, hmt, hallm⟩ := outer_step houter hop hstack hall\n              obtain ⟨t, ht, htt, hallt⟩ := ih htail hrest hmt hallm\n              exact ⟨t, exec_append_iff.mpr ⟨m, hm, ht⟩, htt, hallt⟩\n\n/-- A first-order typed stack never holds a program value, so the observable\nfragment's inputs are leaf stacks. -/\ntheorem firstOrder_of_observable {v : Value} {ty : Ty} (hty : ObservableTy ty)\n    (hv : HasType v ty) : FirstOrderValue v := by\n  induction hty generalizing v with\n  | unit => cases hv with | unit => exact .unit\n  | bool => cases hv with | bool => exact .bool\n  | i64 => cases hv with | i64 => exact .i64\n  | text => cases hv with | text => exact .text\n  | pair ha hb iha ihb =>\n      obtain ⟨x, y, rfl, hx, hy⟩ := hasType_pair_shape hv\n      exact .pair (iha hx) (ihb hy)\n  | sum ha hb iha ihb =>\n      rcases hasType_sum_shape hv with ⟨x, rfl, hx⟩ | ⟨y, rfl, hy⟩\n      · exact .inl (iha hx)\n      · exact .inr (ihb hy)\n  | list ha iha =>\n      obtain ⟨vs, rfl, hvs⟩ := hasType_list_shape hv\n      exact .list (fun x hx => iha (hvs x hx))\n\ntheorem observable_allLeaf {s : Stack} {i : List Ty} (hs : StackTyped s i)\n    (hobs : ∀ t ∈ i, ObservableTy t) : AllLeaf s := by\n  induction hs with\n  | nil => exact allLeaf_nil\n  | cons hv _ ih =>\n      exact allLeaf_cons\n        (firstOrder_leafValue (firstOrder_of_observable (hobs _ (by simp)) hv))\n        (ih (fun t ht => hobs t (by simp [ht])))\n\n/-! ## The four-version relation -/\n\n/-- A normal return of `p` on the public input stack `xs` at the declared\noutput stack type. -/\ndef Observes (outputTys : List Ty) (p : List Op) (xs out : Stack) : Prop :=\n  Exec p xs out ∧ StackTyped out outputTys\n\n/-- `SMERGE/1`: for every well-typed input vector the four subjects return\nnormally, the merge is defined at every output position, and every normal\nreturn of the candidate is the merge of the variants' normal returns.  The\nrelation quantifies over all normal returns, so it never chooses one output of\na nondeterministic evaluator; each fixture subject's uniqueness is proved by\nits `exec_*_unique` lemma, and no general determinism lemma is stated. -/\ndef MergeRel (inputTys outputTys : List Ty) (O A B M : List Op) : Prop :=\n  ∀ xs, StackTyped xs inputTys →\n    (∃ mo, Observes outputTys O xs mo) ∧\n    (∃ ma, Observes outputTys A xs ma) ∧\n    (∃ mb, Observes outputTys B xs mb) ∧\n    (∃ mm, Observes outputTys M xs mm) ∧\n    (∀ mo ma mb mm, Observes outputTys O xs mo → Observes outputTys A xs ma →\n      Observes outputTys B xs mb → Observes outputTys M xs mm →\n      MergeStack mo ma mb mm)\n\ntheorem mergeRel_returns {i o : List Ty} {O A B M : List Op} (h : MergeRel i o O A B M)\n    {xs : Stack} (hxs : StackTyped xs i) :\n    (∃ mo, Observes o O xs mo) ∧ (∃ ma, Observes o A xs ma) ∧\n      (∃ mb, Observes o B xs mb) ∧ (∃ mm, Observes o M xs mm) :=\n  ⟨(h xs hxs).1, (h xs hxs).2.1, (h xs hxs).2.2.1, (h xs hxs).2.2.2.1⟩\n\ntheorem mergeRel_values {i o : List Ty} {O A B M : List Op} (h : MergeRel i o O A B M)\n    {xs : Stack} (hxs : StackTyped xs i) :\n    ∀ mo ma mb mm, Observes o O xs mo → Observes o A xs ma → Observes o B xs mb →\n      Observes o M xs mm → MergeStack mo ma mb mm :=\n  (h xs hxs).2.2.2.2\n\n/-- The relation is symmetric in the two variants: no variant order decides. -/\ntheorem mergeRel_symm {i o : List Ty} {O A B M : List Op} (h : MergeRel i o O A B M) :\n    MergeRel i o O B A M := by\n  intro xs hxs\n  obtain ⟨hO, hA, hB, hM, hvalues⟩ := h xs hxs\n  exact ⟨hO, hB, hA, hM, fun mo mb ma mm hO' hB' hA' hM' =>\n    mergeStack_comm (hvalues mo ma mb mm hO' hA' hB' hM')⟩\n\n/-- A fragment subject's four normal returns are established by the\nterminating-fragment theorem, so the relation is never vacuous for an admitted\nsubject. -/\ntheorem mergeRel_terminating {i o : List Ty} {O A B M : List Op}\n    (hinput : ∀ t ∈ i, ObservableTy t)\n    (hO : CodeTyped O i o) (hFO : OuterCode O)\n    (hA : CodeTyped A i o) (hFA : OuterCode A)\n    (hB : CodeTyped B i o) (hFB : OuterCode B)\n    (hM : CodeTyped M i o) (hFM : OuterCode M) {xs : Stack}\n    (hxs : StackTyped xs i) :\n    (∃ mo, Observes o O xs mo) ∧ (∃ ma, Observes o A xs ma) ∧\n      (∃ mb, Observes o B xs mb) ∧ (∃ mm, Observes o M xs mm) := by\n  have hall := observable_allLeaf hxs hinput\n  obtain ⟨mo, hmo, htmo, _⟩ := outer_exec hO hFO hxs hall\n  obtain ⟨ma, hma, htma, _⟩ := outer_exec hA hFA hxs hall\n  obtain ⟨mb, hmb, htmb, _⟩ := outer_exec hB hFB hxs hall\n  obtain ⟨mm, hmm, htmm, _⟩ := outer_exec hM hFM hxs hall\n  exact ⟨⟨mo, hmo, htmo⟩, ⟨ma, hma, htma⟩, ⟨mb, hmb, htmb⟩, ⟨mm, hmm, htmm⟩⟩\n\n/-- A conflict at one validated input and position refutes any candidate: the\nrelation must hold for the observations, and no value satisfies a conflicting\nposition. -/\ntheorem mergeRel_conflict_absurd {i o : List Ty} {O A B M : List Op}\n    (h : MergeRel i o O A B M) {xs : Stack} (hxs : StackTyped xs i)\n    {mo ma mb mm : Stack} (hmO : Observes o O xs mo) (hmA : Observes o A xs ma)\n    (hmB : Observes o B xs mb) (hmM : Observes o M xs mm)\n    {n : Nat} {ov av bv mv : Value} (ho : mo[n]? = some ov) (ha : ma[n]? = some av)\n    (hb : mb[n]? = some bv) (hm : mm[n]? = some mv) (conflict : Conflict ov av bv) :\n    False :=\n  conflict mv (mergeStack_position (mergeRel_values h hxs mo ma mb mm hmO hmA hmB hmM)\n    ho ha hb hm)\n\ntheorem codeTyped_append {a b : List Op} {i m o : List Ty} (ha : CodeTyped a i m)\n    (hb : CodeTyped b m o) : CodeTyped (a ++ b) i o := by\n  revert i m o\n  induction a with\n  | nil =>\n      intro i m o ha hb\n      cases ha with\n      | nil => exact hb\n  | cons op ops ih =>\n      intro i m o ha hb\n      cases ha with\n      | cons h1 h2 => exact .cons h1 (ih h2 hb)\n\n/-! ## Fixture subjects -/\n\ndef incrementOne : List Op := [.lit (.i64 1), .word 4]\ndef incrementTwo : List Op := [.lit (.i64 2), .word 4]\ndef incrementBottom : List Op := [.word 2, .lit (.i64 1), .word 4, .word 2]\ndef incrementBoth : List Op := incrementBottom ++ incrementOne\ndef identityCode : List Op := []\n\ntheorem typed_identity : CodeTyped identityCode [.i64, .i64] [.i64, .i64] := .nil\n\ntheorem typed_incrementOne : CodeTyped incrementOne [.i64, .i64] [.i64, .i64] :=\n  CodeTyped.cons (m := .nil)\n\ntheorem stackTyped_i64_pair {s : Stack} (h : StackTyped s [.i64, .i64]) :\n    ∃ x y, s = [.i64 x, .i64 y] := by\n  obtain ⟨x0, y0, rfl, hx, hy⟩ := stackTyped_pair_shape h\n  obtain ⟨x, rfl⟩ (i64_value_eq.mp hv)\n\ntheorem not_mergeVal_of_distinct {o a b m : Value} (ha : a ≠ o) (hb : b ≠ o)\n    (hab : a ≠ b) : ¬ MergeVal o a b m := by\n  intro h\n  cases h with\n  | unchanged => exact ha rfl\n  | first _ => exact hb rfl\n  | second _ => exact ha rfl\n  | agree _ _ same => exact hab same\n\n/-! ### `incrementOne` on `[I64 I64]` -/\n\ntheorem exec_incrementOne {x y : BitVec 64} :\n    Exec incrementOne [.i64 x, .i64 y] [.i64 x, .i64 (y + 1)] := by\n  have hlit : Exec [.lit (.i64 1)] [.i64 x, .i64 y] [.i64 x, .i64 y, .i64 1] :=\n    (exec_lit_iff (v := .i64 1) (s := [.i64 x, .i64 : BitVec 64} {t : Stack}\n    (h : Exec incrementBottom [.i64 x, .i64 y] t) :\n    t = [.i64 (x + 1), .i64 y] := by\n  have h' : Exec ([.word 2] ++ [.lit (.i64 1), .word 4, .word 2]) [.i64 x, .i64 y]    Exec incrementBoth [.i64 x, .i64 y] [.i64 (x + 1), .i64 (y + 1)] :=\n  (exec_append_iff (a := incrementBottom) (b := incrementOne)).mpr\n    ⟨_, exec_incrementBottom, exec_incrementOne⟩\n\ntheorem -/\n\n/-- SMERGE-CASE-04: both variants change identically, so the candidate equals\nboth. -/\ntheorem mergeRel_identical_increment :\n    MergeRel [.i64, .i64] [.i64, .i64] identityCode incrementOne incrementOne\n      incrementOne := by\n  intro xs hxs\n  obtain ⟨x, y, rfl⟩ := stackTyped_i64_pair hxs\n  refine ⟨⟨_, exec_nil_iff.mpr rfl, typed_i64_pair⟩,\n    ⟨_, exec_incrementOne, typed_i64_pair⟩,\n    ⟨_, exec_incrementOne, typed_i64_pair⟩,\n    ⟨_, exec_incrementOne, typed_i64_pair⟩, ?_⟩\n  intro mo ma mb mm ho ha hb hm\n  have hmo : mo = [.i64 x, .i64 y] := exec_nil_iff.mp ho.1\n  have hma : ma = [.i64 x, .i64 (y + 1)] := exec_incrementOne_unique ha.1\n  have hmb : mb = [.i64 x, .i64 (y + 1)] := exec_incrementOne_unique hb.1\n  have hmm : mm = [.i64 x, .i64 (y + 1)] := exec_incrementOne_unique hm.1\n  subst hmo; subst hma; subst hmb; subst hmm\n  exact .cons .unchanged (.cons\n    (.agree (i64_ne_of_ne (add_one_ne_self y)) (i64_ne_of_ne (add_one_ne_self y)) rfl)\n    .nil)\n\n/-- SMERGE-CASE-01: independent changes to the two positions are both\npreserved. -/\ntheorem mergeRel_independent_changes :\n    MergeRel [.i64, .i64] [.i64, .i64] identityCode incrementOne incrementBottom\n      incrementBoth := by\n  intro xs hxs\n  obtain ⟨x, y, rfl⟩ := stackTyped_i64_pair hxs\n  refine ⟨⟨_, exec_nil_iff.mpr rfl, typed_i64_pair⟩,\n    ⟨_, exec_incrementOne, typed_i64_pair⟩,\n    ⟨_, exec_incrementBottom, typed_i64_pair⟩,\n    ⟨_, exec_incrementBoth, typed_i64_pair⟩, ?_⟩\n  intro mo ma mb mm ho ha hb hm\n  have hmo : mo = [.i64 x, .i64 y] := exec_nil_iff.mp ho.1\n  have hma : ma = [.i64 x, .i64 (y + 1)] := exec_incrementOne_unique ha.1\n  have hmb : mb = [.i64 (x + 1), .i64 y] := exec_incrementBottom_unique hb.1\n  have hmm : mm = [.i64 (x + 1), .i64 (y + 1)] := exec_incrementBoth_unique hm.1\n  subst hmo; subst hma; subst hmb; subst hmm\n  exact .cons (.second (i64_ne_of_ne (add_one_ne_self x)))\n    (.cons (.first (i64_ne_of_ne (add_one_ne_self y))) .nil)\n\n/-- SMERGE-CASE-07: incompatible changes to one position form a conflict. -/\ntheorem conflict_incompatible_increments (y : BitVec 64) :\n    Conflict (.i64 y) (.i64 (y + 1)) (.i64 (y + 2)) :=\n  fun m => not_mergeVal_of_distinct (m := m) (i64_ne_of_ne (add_one_ne_self y))\n    (i64_ne_of_ne (add_two_ne_self y)) (i64_ne_of_ne (add_one_ne_add_two y))\n\ntheorem Quotations, program\nvalues and the higher-order words are outside the profile. -/\ndef Sequential : List NamedV1.Op → Prop\n  | [] => True\n  | .lit value :: rest => Scalar value ∧ Sequential rest\n  | .word id :: rest =>\n      (id = 0 ∨ id = 1 ∨ id = 2 ∨ id = 4 ∨ id = 5 ∨ id = 6 ∨ id = 7) ∧ Sequential rest\n  | .call _ :: rest => Sequential rest\n  | .block _ :: _rest => False\n\n/-- Every call occurrence of the code resolves in the table. This is the\nprofile's static resolution obligation; it is what `NamedV1.ExactClosure` and\nan accepted source submission establish for real code. -/\ninductive CallsResolve (callee : NamedV1.Environment) : List NamedV1.Op → Prop where\n  | nil : CallsResolve callee []\n  | lit {value rest} : CallsResolve callee rest → CallsResolve callee (.lit value :: rest)\n  | word {id rest} : CallsResolve callee rest → CallsResolve callee (.word id :: rest)\n  | block {body rest} : CallsResolve callee rest → CallsResolve callee (.block body :: rest)\n  | call {use rest} : (∃ target, callee.lookup use.slot = some target) →\n      CallsResolve callee rest → CallsResolve callee (.call use :: rest)\n\n/-- Static resolution of every call occurrence follows from the closure facts\n`NamedV1.ExactClosure` binds, for any profile body. -/\ntheorem callsResolve_of_resolves {env : NamedV1.Environment} :\n    ∀ {code : List NamedV1.Op}, Sequential code →\n      (∀ use ∈ NamedV1.usesCode code, ∃ target, env.lookup use.slot = some target) →\n      CallsResolve env code := by\n  intro code\n  induction code with\n  | nil => intro _ _; exact .nil\n  | cons op rest ih =>\n      intro profile codeResolved\n      cases op with\n      | lit value =>\n          exact .lit (ih profile.2 fun use hu => codeResolved use (by simp [NamedV1.usesCode, hu]))\n      | word id =>\n          exact .word (ih profile.2 fun use hu => codeResolved use (by simp [NamedV1.usesCode, hu]))\n      | block body => exact profile.elim\n      | call use =>\n          exact .call (codeResolved use (by simp [NamedV1.usesCode, NamedV1.usesOp]))\n            (ih profile fun use hu => codeResolved use (by simp [NamedV1.usesCode, hu]))\n\n/-- T3 (`embed`), backward half on the bounded profile: when the callee\nresolves every call occurrence, and every body is a profile body, the caller\ncannot invent a different normal result. entry.body) :\n    ∀ {code : List NamedV1.Op} {stack final : NamedV1.Stack}, Sequential code →\n      CallsResolve callee code →\n      NamedV1.Run caller code stack final → NamedV1.Run callee code stack final := by\n  intro code stack final codeProfile codeResolved execution\n  revert codeProfile codeResolved\n  apply NamedV1.Run.recOn\n    (motive_1 := fun code s t _ => Sequential code → CallsResolve callee code →\n      NamedV1.Run callee code s t)\n    (motive_2 := fun op s t _ => Sequential [op] → CallsResolve callee [op] →\n      NamedV1.Step callee op s t)\n    (t := execution)\n ⟨codeProfile.1, trivial⟩ (.lit .nil))\n              (‹Sequential ops → CallsResolve callee ops → NamedV1.Run callee ops m t›\n                codeProfile.2 hrest)\n    | word id =>\n        cases all_goals intros\n  all_goals first\n    | (cases ‹CallsResolve callee [NamedV1.Op.call _]› with\n       | call huse hrest =>\n           obtain ⟨entry₀, hlookup₀⟩ := huse\n           have callerLookup := ‹caller.lookup _ = some _›\n           have callerMatches := ‹NamedV1.Entry.matches _ _›\n           have mem₀ : entry₀ ∈ callee.entries := lookup_entries hlookup₀\n           have      have entryEq := Option.some.inj (callerRow'.symm.trans callerLookup)\n           cases entryEq\n           refine .call hlookup₀ callerMatches ?_\n           exact ‹_ → _ → NamedV1.Run callee _ _ [Sequential])\n\n\n/-- T3 (`embed`): the frozen snapshot is exact on the bounded profile. The\ncaller and the callee execute the accepted body to the same normal results; no\nstack, value or body is renamed, because a call occurrence inside an accepted\nbody is a source node of the callee module and keeps its table identity, and a\nspecialization slot is fresh in the callee's own table. -/\ntheorem embed {callee caller : NamedV1.Environment} (e : Embedding callee caller)\n    (rows : Resolves callee) (bodies : ∀ entry ∈ callee.entries, Sequential entry.body)\n    {code : List NamedV1.Op} (profile : Sequential code) (resolved : CallsResolve callee code)\n    {stack final : NamedV1.Stack} :\n    NamedV1.Run callee code stack final ↔ NamedV1.Run caller code stack final :=\n  ⟨run_forward e.snapshot, run_backward e.snapshot rows bodies profile resolved⟩\n\n/-- T2 (`callFrame`): an entry embedding plus the callee's exported claim give\nthe caller's frame discipline for one call occurrence. The claim is used at the\ncaller's own `tail`; no callee body is re-derived and no second claim form is\nintroduced. The profile and resolution premises are the bounded profile's\nstatic obligations for the callee's own code. -/\ntheorem callFrame {caller : NamedV1.Environment} (subject : NamedV1.Subject)\n    (entry : NamedV1.Entry) (use : NamedV1.Use)\n    (e : EntryEmbedding caller subject entry use)\n    (pre post : NamedV1.Stack → NamedV1.Stack → NamedV1.Stack → Prop)\n    (claim : NamedV2.exportedNamedClaim subject [] pre post)\n    (bodies : ∀ row ∈ subject.env.entries, Sequential row.body)\n    (profile : Sequential subject.root) :\n    ∀ frame args final,\n      NamedV2.StackTyped subject.env args entry.input →\n      pre args [] [] →\n      NamedV1.Exec caller [.call use] (frame ++ args) final →\n      ∃ res, final = frame ++ res ∧\n        NamedV2.StackTyped subject.env res entry.output ∧ post args res [] := by\n  intro frame args final hargs hpre execution\n  have run : NamedV1.Run caller [.call use] (frame ++ args).reverse final.reverse := execution\n  cases run with\n  | cons step rest =>\n      cases step with\n      | call lookupCaller matchesCaller inner =>\n          cases rest with\n          | nil =>\n              rw [e.lookup] at lookupCaller\n              injection lookupCaller with sameEntry\n              rw [← sameEntry, e.body] at inner\n              have innerCallee : NamedV1.Run subject.env subject.root\n                  (frame ++ args).reverse final.reverse :=\n                (embed e.embedding (Subject.resolves subject) bodies profile\n                  (callsResolve_of_resolves profile (Subject.root_resolves subject))).mpr inner\n              rw [e.input] at hargs\n              obtain ⟨res, hfinal, htyped, hpost⟩ :=\n                claim frame args [] hargs .nil hpre final innerCallee\n              exact ⟨res, hfinal, by rw [e.output]; exact htyped, hpost⟩\n\n\n/-- One justified call in a chain: a resolved occurrence of an accepted callee\nwith its `callFrame` data, plus the caller's own bridges. `bridge` is the pure\nlogical step into the callee's precondition (with the callee-side argument\ntyping the claim needs); `join` is the caller's step out of the callee's\npostcondition. Neither field can smuggle a program call: both are propositions\nabout the caller's own assertion, never a `pc-call` slice. -/\nstructure ChainStep (caller : NamedV1.Environment) (P Q : NamedV1.Stack → Prop) where\n  subject : NamedV1.Subject\n  entry : NamedV1.Entry\n  use : NamedV1.Use\n  embedding : EntryEmbedding caller subject entry use\n  bodies : ∀ row ∈ subject.env.entries, Sequential row.body\n  profile : Sequential subject.root\n  pre : NamedV1.Stack → NamedV1.Stack → NamedV1.Stack → Prop\n  post : NamedV1.Stack → NamedV1.Stack → NamedV1.Stack → Prop\n  claim : NamedV2.exportedNamedClaim subject [] pre post\n  /-- the caller's bridge into the callee precondition and argument typing -/\n  bridge : ∀ args, P args →\n    pre args [] [] ∧ NamedV2.StackTyped subject.env args entry.input\n  /-- the caller's join out of the callee postcondition -/\n  join : ∀ args res, P args → post args res [] →\n    NamedV2.StackTyped subject.env res entry.output → Q res\n\n/-- A chain of independently justified call steps: the empty chain is the\nidentity on the caller's assertion, and a step composes with the rest through\nits own `join`. -/\ninductive Chain (caller : NamedV1.Environment) : (NamedV1.Stack → Prop) →\n    (NamedV1.Stack → Prop) → Type where\n  | nil (P) : Chain caller P P\n  | cons {P Q R} : ChainStep caller P Q → Chain caller Q R → Chain caller P R\n\n/-- The caller code a chain justifies: exactly one call per step, in order. -/\ndef Chain.code {caller : NamedV1.Environment} {P Q : NamedV1.Stack → Prop} :\n    Chain caller P Q → List NamedV1.Op\n  | .nil _ => []\n  | .cons step rest => .call step.use :: rest.code\n\n@[simp] theorem run_nil_iff {env : NamedV1.Environment} {s t : NamedV1.Stack} :\n    NamedV1.Run env [] s t ↔ t = s := by\n  constructor\n  · intro h\n    cases h\n    rfl\n  · intro h\n    subst h\n    exact .nil\n\n/-- T5 (`chainClaim`) execution half: a chain of call steps, each justified by\nits resolved entry and the callee's own claim, transports the caller's\nassertion through the whole sequence. No callee body is re-derived. -/\ntheorem chainExec {caller : NamedV1.Environment} :\n    ∀ {P Q : NamedV1.Stack → Prop}, (chain : Chain caller P Q) →\n      ∀ tail args final, P args →\n        NamedV1.Exec caller (Chain.code chain) (tail ++ args) final →\n        ∃ res, final = tail ++ res ∧ Q res := by\n  intro P Q chain\n  induction chain with\n  | nil P =>\n      intro tail args final hP execution\n      refine ⟨args, ?_, hP⟩\n      have empty : final.reverse = (tail ++ args).reverse := by\n        have h : NamedV1.Run caller [] ((tail ++ args).reverse) final.reverse := by\n          simpa [NamedV1.Exec, Chain.code] using execution\n        simpa using (run_nil_iff.mp h)\n      simpa using congrArg List.reverse empty\n  | @cons P Q R step rest ih =>\n      intro tail args final hP execution\n      obtain ⟨mid, hcall, hrest⟩ :=\n        (exec_append_iff (a := [NamedV1.Op.call step.use]) (b := Chain.code rest)).mp\n          (by simpa [Chain.code] using execution)\n      obtain ⟨res, hmid, htyped, hpost⟩ :=\n        callFrame step.subject step.entry step.use step.embedding step.pre step.post\n          step.claim step.bodies step.profile\n          tail args mid (step.bridge args hP).2 (step.bridge args hP).1 hcall\n      obtain ⟨res', hfinal, hQ⟩ :=\n        ih tail res final (step.join args res hP hpost htyped)\n          (by rw [← hmid]; exact hrest)\n      exact ⟨res', hfinal, hQ⟩\n\n/-- T5 (`chainClaim`): a chain of independently justified call steps implies the\ncaller's exported named claim. The two-step rule re-derived both callee bodies;\nhere each step is one resolved entry plus the callee's own claim. The landed\nshape is narrower than \"n-ary generalization of `twoStepClaim`\" and the design\namendment of 2026-11-20 records it: `Chain.code` emits only calls (no literal or\nbuiltin segments, so S04's `[ swap d.sub2 ]` is not expressible yet), each step\nconsumes the whole stack above the chain's single fixed `tail`, and the post is\noutput-only (`post := fun _ after _ => Q after`), so a relational claim such as\n`out0 = in0 + 2` needs per-instance glue. -/\ntheorem chainClaim (subject : NamedV1.Subject) {P Q : NamedV1.Stack → Prop}\n    (chain : Chain subject.env P Q) (root : subject.root = Chain.code chain)\n    (typed : ∀ res, Q res → NamedV2.StackTyped subject.env res subject.output) :\n    NamedV2.exportedNamedClaim subject []\n      (fun before _ _ => P before) (fun _ after _ => Q after) := by\n  intro tail before params _ _ hpre final execution\n  rw [root] at execution\n  obtain ⟨res, hfinal, hQ⟩ := chainExec chain tail before final hpre execution\n  exact ⟨res, hfinal, typed res hQ, hQ⟩\n\n\n/-! ## T4: the MC1 claim lifts along `liftCode` -/\n\n/-- A scalar MC1 value: the literals the bounded profile admits. -/\ndef ScalarMC1 : NobleContracts.Value → Prop\n  | .unit | .bool _ | .i64 _ | .text _ => True\n  | .pair _ _ | .inl _ | .inr _ | .list _ | .program _ | .«syntax» _ => False\n\n/-- MC1 profile code: scalar literals and the pure scalar builtins. MC1 has no\nnamed calls; quotations and program values are outside the profile. -/\ndef ProfileCode : List NobleContracts.Op → Prop\n  | [] => True\n  | .lit value :: rest => ScalarMC1 value ∧ NamedV1.liftStack ([] : NobleContracts.Stack) = [] := rfl\n\ntheorem liftValue_i64_iff {v : NobleContracts.Value} {n : BitVec 64} :\n    NamedV1.liftValue v = .i64 n ↔ v = .i64 n := by\n  cases v <;> Logical parameters are not executable\ninputs.  `pre` sees no output stack.  The arbitrary untouched bottom prefix is\nquantified outside the execution, and a normal result must preserve that prefix\nand have the declared, exact output types. -/\ndef exportedClaim (code : List Op) (inputTypes outputTypes paramTypes : List Ty)\n    (pre post : Stack → Stack → Stack → Prop) : Prop :=\n  ∀ tail before params,\n    StackTyped before inputTypes → StackTyped params paramTypes →\n    pre before [] params →\n    ∀ final, Exec code (tail ++ before) final →\n      ∃ after, final = tail ++ after ∧ StackTyped after outputTypes ∧\n        post before after params\n\n/-- Keep the output boundary while strengthening the input assumption and\nweakening the postcondition.  The implications are logical, not textual. -/\ntheorem exportedClaim_consequence {code inputTypes outputTypes paramTypes pre post pre' post'}\n    (proof : exportedClaim code inputTypes outputTypes paramTypes pre post)\n    (hpre : ∀ before params, pre' before [] params → pre before [] params)\n    (hpost : ∀ before after params,\n      pre' before [] params → post before after params → post' before after params) :\n    exportedClaim code inputTypes outputTypes paramTypes pre' post' := by\n  intro tail before params hi hp hreq final he\n  obtain ⟨after, hout, ht, hensure⟩ := proof tail before params hi hp (hpre _ _ hreq) final he\n  exact ⟨after, hout, ht, hpost _ _ _ hreq hensure⟩\n\n/-- An exact semantic rule is enough for the corresponding `maps` assertion.\nUnlike this helper's premise, `Maps` itself makes no termination assertion. -/\ntheorem maps_of_exec_iff {code arguments expected}\n    (rule : ∀ actual, Exec code arguments actual ↔ actual = expected) :\n    Maps (.program code) arguments expected := by\n  intro actual he\n  exact (rule actual).mp he\n\n/-- Composition of higher-order behavior specifications uses the exact\nintermediate value.  The second program's input cannot be silently replaced by\nan unrelated precondition of the same type. -/\ntheorem maps_append {first second input middle output}\n    (left : Maps (.program first) input middle)\n    (right : Maps (.program second) middle output) :\n    Maps (.program (first ++ second)) input output := by\n  intro actual he\n  obtain ⟨mid, hfirst, hsecond⟩ := exec_append_iff.mp he\n  have hmid := left mid hfirst\n  subst mid\n  exact right actual hsecond\n\n/-- A convenient bridge for universally quantified, tail-preserving I64\nfunctions. A refutation is therefore one\nconcrete normal-return execution on one concrete input whose final stack\nviolates the postcondition. The host supplies the input; the kernel checks the\nexecution and the violated predicate. Nothing in this module searches for a\ncounterexample, reflects on decidable goals, or invokes a tactic.\n\nThe template is derived from the reviewed model only: `exportedClaim` (the\npublic boundary), `Exec` (normal-return execution), and `Holds`/`evaluate`\n(assertion semantics over BitVec-64 wrapping arithmetic). It adds no axiom and\nno new trusted component. Two reviewed instances are included: the\n`monotonic` fixture at the positive signed boundary, and a wrapping program\nconstant that turns the smallest positive input into the minimum value.\n\nScope, stated explicitly: partial correctness on *normal returns* only. A\ntrap, a non-terminating program, an effectful word, or a compiler/runtime\ndisagreement is outside what this template can refute, and a compiled\nobservation is separate evidence (`observed-execution`), never a premise here.\n-/\n\nnamespace NobleContracts\n\n/-! ## Kernel-checked evaluation helpers\n\nThese small lemmas turn a computed `false` evaluation into the negation of\n`Holds`. They are stated over the definitions, so an instance needs no tactic:\n`Holds` and `evaluate` reduce definitionally on concrete values, and only the\nfinal `Option`/`Value` constructor conflict needs a proof. -/\n\n/-- The two `Bool` constructors cannot be equal. -/\ntheorem value_bool_false_ne_true : ¬ (Value.bool false = Value.bool true) :=\n  fun equal => Eq.mp Bool.false_eq_true (Eq.mp (Value.bool.injEq false true) equal)\n\n/-- `some` preserves the `Bool` constructor conflict. -/\ntheorem some_value_bool_false_ne_true :\n    ¬ (some (Value.bool false) = some (Value.bool true)) :=\n  fun equal => value_bool_false_ne_true (Option.some.inj equal)\n\n/-- A concrete term that evaluates to `(bool false)` does not hold. The\n    evaluation equation is the instance's computed input/output; it is not a\n    proof search or a reflection oracle. -/\ntheorem not_holds_of_evaluate_false {term : Term} {before after params : Stack}\n    (evaluated : evaluate term ⟨before, after, params⟩ = some (Value.bool false)) :\n    ¬ Holds term before after params :=\n  fun holds => some_value_bool_false_ne_true (Eq.trans (Eq.symm evaluated) holds)\n\n/-! ## The template -/\n\n/-- A concrete unary-`I64` counterexample refutes the exported claim.\n\n`run` is the *checked* normal-return execution of the contract's exact code on\nthe exact input `input` and output `output`; `preHolds` is the asserted\nprecondition at that input; `postFails` is the violated postcondition at the\nsame observations. No other premise is needed: the claim's quantifier is\ninstantiated at the empty prefix, the single typed input, and no parameters,\nand the existential suffix it demands is the concrete output stack.\n\nThe proof is a term: the claim is applied once to the concrete execution, and\nthe resulting `Holds post` is contradicted by `postFails` after the claim's own\nsuffix equation identifies the demanded suffix with the concrete output. -/\ntheorem exportedClaim_unaryI64_refutation {code : List Op} {pre post : Term}\n    {input output : BitVec 64}\n    (run : Exec code [.i64 input] [.i64 output])\n    (preHolds : Holds pre [.i64 input] [] [])\n    (postFails : ¬ Holds post [.i64 input] [.i64 output] []) :\n    ¬ exportedClaim code [.i64] [.i64] [] (Holds pre) (Holds post) :=\n  fun claim =>\n    match claim [] [.i64 input] [] (StackTyped.cons HasType.i64 StackTyped.nil)\n        StackTyped.nil preHolds [.i64 output] run with\n    | ⟨_after, hfinal, _, hpost⟩ => (hfinal ▸ postFails) hpost\n\n/-! ## Instance: the `monotonic` fixture\n\n`verification/mc1/monotonic.noble-contract` declares `[ 1 + ]` with\n`(ensures (lt (in x) (out y)))`. At the maximum signed input the wrapping add\nreturns the minimum signed value, so `(lt (in x) (out y))` is false. The program\nlist and the postcondition term below are the ones the frontend emits for that\nfixture (`program`, `precondition` and `postcondition` in the generated\n`MC1Obligation` module are transparent definitions of these values). -/\n\n/-- The exported `program` of the `monotonic` fixture: the word list `[ 1 + ]`. -/\ndef monotonic_program : List Op := [.lit (.i64 (BitVec.ofInt 64 1)), .word 4]\n\n/-- The counterexample input: the maximum signed 64-bit value. -/\ndef monotonic_input : BitVec 64 := BitVec.ofInt 64 9223372036854775807\n\n/-- The observed output: wrapping `input + 1`, i.e. the minimum signed value. -/\ndef monotonic_output : BitVec 64 := monotonic_input + BitVec.ofInt 64 1\n\n/-- The concrete normal-return execution, built from the reviewed primitive\n    rules (`exec_lit_iff` then `exec_add_iff`) and their sequencing. -/\ntheorem monotonic_counterexample_run :\n    Exec monotonic_program [.i64 monotonic_input] [.i64 monotonic_output] :=\n  exec_append_iff.mpr\n    ⟨[.i64 monotonic_input, .i64 (BitVec.ofInt 64 1)],\n      exec_lit_iff (v := .i64 (BitVec.ofInt 64 1)) (s := [.i64 monotonic_input]) |>.mpr rfl,\n      exec_add_iff (a := monotonic_input) (b := BitVec.ofInt 64 1) (s := []) |>.mpr rfl⟩\n\n/-- At the boundary the postcondition `(lt (in x) (out y))` evaluates to false:\n    the maximum signed value is not below the minimum signed value. -/\ntheorem monotonic_postcondition_false :\n    ¬ Holds (.lt (.input 0) (.output 0)) [.i64 monotonic_input] [.i64 monotonic_output] [] :=\n  not_holds_of_evaluate_false rfl\n\n/-- The `monotonic` fixture's exported claim is refuted at the maximum input. -/\ntheorem monotonic_refutation :\n    ¬ exportedClaim monotonic_program [.i64] [.i64] []\n        (Holds (.bool true)) (Holds (.lt (.input 0) (.output 0))) :=\n  exportedClaim_unaryI64_refutation monotonic_counterexample_run\n    (holds_true [.i64 monotonic_input] [] []) monotonic_postcondition_false\n\n/-! ## Instance: a wrapping program constant\n\nThe same postcondition with a different unary program, `[ 9223372036854775807 + ]`,\nat input `1`. The program constant and the input are both boundary values, and\ntheir wrapping sum is the minimum signed value, so the claim that the result is\nstrictly above the input fails. rfl\n\n/-- The wrapping-program claim is refuted at the smallest positive input. -/\ntheorem wrap_constant_refutation :\n    ¬ exportedClaim wrap_constant_program [.i64] [.i64] []\n        (Holds (.bool true)) (Holds (.lt (.input 0) (.output 0))) :=\n  exportedClaim_unaryI64_refutation wrap_constant_counterexample_run\n    (holds_true [.i64 wrap_constant_input] [] []) Specification-quality claims kept strictly separate from implementation\nsatisfaction (`issue 65`). These are claims about the *retained relation*, not\nabout any implementation, and a relational claim here never asserts executable\ntermination, absence of traps, success under quotas, or intended meaning.\n\nBounded profile: pure `I64`/`Bool` exact ordered stacks, the existing predicate\nsemantics and wrapping arithmetic. `maps`, program/syntax values, resources,\neffects, concurrency and arbitrary recursion are out of scope. -/\nnamespace NobleContracts.SpecQuality\nopen NobleContracts\n\n/-- One retained relation: exact ordered input/output/parameter types plus the\ntwo predicate stacks. The implementation claim is deliberately not a field, so\nno specification-quality obligation can be discharged by an implementation\nproof. -/\nstructure Spec where\n  code : List Op\n  input : List Ty\n  output : List Ty\n  params : List Ty\n  pre : Stack → Stack → Stack → Prop\n  post : Stack → Stack → Stack → Prop\n\n/-- An admissible assignment: both stacks are exactly typed and the\nprecondition holds. Ghost parameters never enter execution. -/\ndef Admissible (spec : Spec) (before params : Stack) : Prop :=\n  StackTyped before spec.input ∧ StackTyped params spec.params ∧ spec.pre before [] params\n\n/-- 1. Domain inhabitation: some well-typed assignment satisfies the precondition. -/\ndef Inhabited (spec : Spec) : Prop :=\n  ∃ before params, Admissible spec before params\n\n/-- 2. This is *not* an execution claim. -/\ndef Realizable (spec : Spec) : Prop :=\n  ∀ before params, Admissible spec before params →\n    ∃ after, StackTyped after spec.output ∧ spec.post before after params\n\n/-- 3. Output determinacy under the exact scalar equality: two satisfying outputs\nfor the same assignment are equal. ### Submitted evidence is derived, not trusted (issue #65 residual)\n\nA doc comment cannot attach to a mutual block, so this section is documented as prose.\nStructural equality of scalar types is an explicit traversal rather than a derived instance, so a\nsubmitted observation relation cannot be read as equal to the retained one by an instance that\nsilently changes. -/\nmutual\n  def sameType : Ty → Ty → Bool\n    | .unit, .unit => true\n    | .bool, .bool => true\n    | .i64, .i64 => true\n    | .text, .text => true\n    | .syntax, .syntax => true\n    | .pair left right, .pair otherLeft otherRight =>\n        sameType left otherLeft && sameType right otherRight\n    | .sum left right, .sum otherLeft otherRight =>\n        sameType left otherLeft && sameType right otherRight\n    | .list item, .list other => sameType item other\n    | .program inputs outputs, .program otherInputs otherOutputs =>\n        sameTypes inputs otherInputs && sameTypes outputs otherOutputs\n    | _, _ => false\n  def sameTypes : List Ty → List Ty → Bool\n    | [], [] => true\n    | head :: tail, other :: otherTail => sameType head other && sameTypes tail otherTail\n    | _, _ => false\nend\n\n/-- A submitted evidence artifact (issue #65 residual): it *claims* which obligation it discharges,\nwhich retained relation it is about — kind, observation relation (input/output types) and parameter\nbinding — and which assignment it asserts. (run_frame r rest)\n\n  /-- One operation leaves every value below the stack it touches unchanged. -/\n  theorem step_frame {op : Op} {s t : Stack} (r : Stack) :\n      Step op s t → Step op (s ++ r) (t ++ r)\n    | .lit => .lit\n    | .block => .block\n    | .dup => .dup\n    | .drop => .drop\n    | .swap => .swap\n    | .dip h => .dip (run_frame r h)\n    | .add => .add\n    | .sub => .sub\n    | .mul => .mul\n    | .equals => .equals\n    | .quote => .quote\n    | .compose => .compose\n    | .run h => .run (run_frame r h)\n    | .reflect => .reflect\n    | .unit => .unit\n    | .pair => .pair\n    | .unpair => .unpair\n    | .inl => .inl\n    | .inr => .inr\n    | .caseLeft h => .caseLeft (run_frame r h)\n    | .caseRight h => .caseRight (run_frame r h)\n    | .ifTrue h => .ifTrue (run_frame r h)\n    | .ifFalse h => .ifFalse (run_frame r h)\n    | .nil => .nil\n    | .cons => .cons\n    | .listNil h => .listNil (run_frame r h)\n    | .listCons h => .listCons (run_frame r h)\nend\n\n/-- **The frame lemma** (public, bottom-first stacks): values below the ones a program uses\npass through it unchanged. -/\ntheorem exec_frame {p : List Op} {s t : Stack} (r : Stack) (h : Exec p s t) :\n    Exec p (r ++ s) (r ++ t) := by\n  unfold Exec at *\n  simpa [List.reverse_append] using run_frame r.reverse h\n\n/-! ## Helpers -/\n\n/-- Rewriting the code after a leading h]\n\n/-- Pushing a block and dipping under it runs the dipped code, then pushes the block. -/\ntheorem run_push_block_dip {q c rest : List Op} {s t : Stack} :\n    Run (.block q :: .block c :: .word 3 :: rest) s t ↔ Run (c ++ .block q :: rest) s t := by\n  simp only [Run.cons_iff, Run.append_iff, Step.block_iff, Step.dip_iff, exists_eq_left]\n  constructor\n  · rintro ⟨_, ⟨u, hc, rfl⟩, hr⟩\n    exact ⟨u, hc, hr⟩\n  · rintro ⟨u, hc, hr⟩\n    exact ⟨_, ⟨u, hc, rfl⟩, hr⟩\n\n/-- `pair` on a variable stack: it needs a second value. -/\ntheorem step_pair_iff {b : Value} {u t : Stack} :\n    Step .pair\n\n/-- `[p] first` on a pair, top-first: `p` runs on the first component with the second hidden. -/\ntheorem run_first_iff {p : List Op} {sr tr : Stack} {a b : Value} :\n    Run (.block p :: exact ⟨c, rest, hp, rfl⟩\n  · rintro ⟨c, rest, hp, rfl⟩\n    exact ⟨_, ⟨_, hp, rfl⟩, _, step_pair_iff.mpr ⟨c, rest, rfl, rfl⟩, rfl⟩\n\n/-- `[p] first` on a pair runs `p` on the pair's first component, hiding the second. -/\ntheorem first_iff {p : List Op} {s : Stack} {a b : Value} {t : Stack} :\n    Exec (.block p :: first) (s ++ [.pair a b]) t ↔\n      ∃ u c, Exec p (s ++ [a]) (u ++ [c]) ∧ t = u ++ [.pair c b] := by\n  simp only [Exec, List.reverse_append, List.reverse_cons, List.reverse_nil, List.nil_append,\n    List.cons_append]\n  rw [run_first_iff]\n  constructor\n  · rintro ⟨c, rest,
